# 10db Takeover ("Open in terminal") Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** The builder presses **Open in terminal** on a running, waiting or failed workstream. 10db pauses its managed Claude session at a safe point, releases the lease and opens the same conversation with `claude --resume <id>` in a terminal inside the 10db window: an xterm.js pane on a PTY the daemon owns. The builder drives it by hand, quits Claude and presses **Hand back**. 10db then reconciles git (branch, history, commits, claims) and resumes the conversation in managed mode; a failed workstream is retried from there. The terminal runs on the project's current account: when the project has moved off the account the conversation ran on, it starts a fresh conversation there, briefed from the step's handoff, and hand-back resumes that one. The session is never driven two ways at once.

**Must build on (this plan executes after all three are on `main`):**

- **Batch delivery**, plan `08-batch-delivery`, merged (#54). This plan uses its `Git.currentBranch`, `BatchDto`, `getBatch`, `transitionBatch`, `UNFINISHED_BATCH_STATES`, `shipReadiness`, the `Shipper`'s `ship`, `shipAnyway`, `abandon` and `checkBatch`, `recoverBatches`, `createDeliveryHarness`, the async `Scheduler.tick()` with `Scheduler.idle()`, the branch check before every launch, `WorkstreamDto.batchId`, and `retryWorkstream` reopening a `FAILED` batch.
- **M2b Part A**, plan `07-m2b-board-and-settings`, merged. Takeover is a board-card action (`views/board/task-card-menu.tsx`, `task-card.tsx`, `board-view.tsx`).
- **Accounts**, plan `09-accounts` (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-10db-09-accounts.md`, on `origin/docs/accounts-plan`), Tasks 1–12 merged. This plan uses `AccountId`, `AccountRow`, `getAccountRow`, `seedAccount`, `patchAccount`, `sessions.account_id` (`SessionRow.accountId`) and `insertSession`'s `accountId` (the account a session launches on, else the project's); `environment/`'s `Launcher` (`session(account, alias): SessionLaunch`, `AccountLaunchError`), built once in `start.ts`; `unready(account)` and `AccountRuntime.blocked(account, projectId)` from `accounts/`; `ProjectDto.accountId` and `patchProject` moving a project with work in flight; the default account ("Claude plan") seeded on the builder's own login; the rule that a lane resuming a conversation from another account starts fresh from its handoff (accounts AC11, `nextActionOnAnotherAccount`, `briefedFromHandoff`); `getSessionByProviderId`; the subscription `StartGate` (accounts AC8) with `TestEngine.startGate`; the monthly-cap card (`capCard`); `Control.requestHandoff` and `SessionState.handoffCause` beside this plan's runner fields; `TestEngine.accounts` and `TestEngine.keychain`; and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 3.

Task 1's first step checks all three are on `main` and stops if one is not.

**Architecture:**

- **States.** `TAKEN_OVER` already exists with the edges `RUNNING → TAKEN_OVER`, `TAKEN_OVER → RECOVERING | CANCELLED`. This plan adds `HANDING_OFF → TAKEN_OVER`, `WAITING_HUMAN → TAKEN_OVER` and `FAILED → TAKEN_OVER`. A workstream stores a `takeover` record while it is taken over: the provider session and the account it ran on, `HEAD`, the branch, the event seq, whether it rescued failed work, and how its last terminal ended. Hand-back goes `TAKEN_OVER → RECOVERING → QUEUED` with a `resume` next action on the same conversation.
- **Safe point.** The runner gains `takeOver(id)`. A live session is sent a notice and stops at the first of: its next tool call (denied before it runs), the end of its turn, a wait on the builder, or 60 seconds (then it is interrupted). The runner then enters `TAKEN_OVER`: lease cleared, claims kept, open question and permission cards cancelled in the same write. A workstream parked on a card, or one that failed, has no runner and moves directly. A takeover is refused while the gate runs, so no request waits on a gate; one that finds no conversation to open lapses at once and the step runs on.
- **The terminal.** The daemon owns a PTY per taken-over workstream. A new `terminal/` module wraps `@lydell/node-pty` (the only module that imports it): it spawns a program, keeps the newest `scrollbackChars` (262 144) of its output in memory, streams output to every attached pane, and ends every terminal at shutdown (SIGHUP, then SIGKILL after `stopGraceMs`). The takeover service spawns `claude` directly, with no shell, in the repo, in the environment the accounts launcher builds for the project's current account (`Launcher.session`): `--resume <id> --model <m> --effort <e> --append-system-prompt <note>` when the conversation ran on that account, else a fresh `--session-id <uuid> … <briefing>` conversation briefed from the step's handoff. On a subscription account it holds the account's start gate until Claude first prints. A terminal session is a `sessions` row with `mode: terminal`. When Claude exits the row ends and the takeover records the exit status; a shutdown or restart ends the process and records why. The workstream stays `TAKEN_OVER` through all of it, and **Open terminal again** resumes the conversation. Closing the pane or the window ends nothing.
- **The channel.** The pane talks to the PTY over a WebSocket on the daemon's own server, `/workstreams/:id/terminal`, with the event stream's auth: the UI token in the query, the allowed-origin check, a listener on `127.0.0.1` only. The pane sends `input` and `resize`; the daemon sends `output` (the scrollback first, then live) and `exit`. Terminal bytes never enter the event log or the database. Nothing in the Tauri shell changes.
- **Hand back.** It is refused while Claude still runs in the terminal (checked before git is read and again inside the hand-back write), when the checkout is detached or on the wrong branch (the batch branch for work in an unfinished batch, else the branch at takeover), and when history no longer contains the takeover `HEAD`. Otherwise every commit since the takeover that no other workstream recorded is credited to this one (`commit.recorded` with `via: 'terminal'`). Committed and dirty paths go through the watcher's step-end claim check, which flags and never blocks. A rescued workstream's hand-back also does what Retry does.
- **Rescue.** A `FAILED` workstream with a started conversation can be taken over when Retry would accept it. The claims it released when it failed are taken back in the takeover's write. Hand back then resets its failure count, restarts its step clean, reopens a `FAILED` batch and resumes the same conversation.
- **Interactions.** One workstream per project can be taken over at a time: a second takeover is refused with the first one's name. Takeover is refused while the workstream's batch is `CUTTING` or `SHIPPING`. A taken-over workstream holds its batch open (`shipReadiness` already calls it blocked) and blocks Abandon (the batch plan's abandon already refuses `TAKEN_OVER`). It holds its claims but no lane, no lease and no account lane-cap slot. Keep-awake holds while its terminal's process runs. The watcher watches it, and its writes inside its own claims are its own for as long as it is taken over. A human-driven terminal session is **not guarded**: no `PreToolUse` guard, no `commit` tool and no 10db MCP server. Its commits are the builder's own git commits, attributed at hand-back.
- **API and UI.** `POST /workstreams/:id/takeover`, `POST /workstreams/:id/hand-back` and the WebSocket `/workstreams/:id/terminal`. The Factory row and the board card menu offer **Open in terminal**, **Show terminal**, **Open terminal again** and **Hand back**, with a note on the terminal's state and a confirmation that names the rules of the shared checkout. The terminal view, `/terminal/$workstreamId`, holds the pane, the note and the same buttons.

**Tech Stack:** As in M2a, the batch delivery plan and the accounts plan, plus three exact pins:

- **`@lydell/node-pty` 1.1.0** (daemon, MIT). A fork of Microsoft's `node-pty` (it is based on `node-pty` 1.1.0-beta14) that ships each platform's prebuilt addon as an optional package (`@lydell/node-pty-darwin-arm64`, `-darwin-x64`, `-linux-x64`, `-linux-arm64`, `-win32-*`, each with `os` and `cpu` set) and has no install script. 1.1.0 is its newest stable release; its `latest` tag points at 1.2.0-beta.15, a beta. Checked on 2026-10-23 with `npm view` and on this Mac (Node 26.12.0, darwin-arm64): it spawns `/bin/sh` in a PTY, resizes it and echoes input. Microsoft's own `node-pty` 1.1.0 (latest stable, MIT, December 2025) was the first choice and fails twice here. Its published darwin `spawn-helper` lacks the execute bit, so every spawn on this Mac failed with `posix_spawnp failed.` (fixed only in the 1.2.0 betas). It also ships no Linux prebuild and falls back to `node-gyp rebuild`, which needs `make` and a C++ compiler that the CI gate's Playwright image doesn't have (decision 87), and an `allowBuilds` entry under `strictDepBuilds`. The fork needs neither.
- **`@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0** and **`@xterm/addon-fit` 0.11.0** (desktop, MIT, both latest stable, released together on 2025-12-22): the renderer spec §10 names, and the addon that sizes it to its pane.
- **The sidecar.** The daemon already ships one native addon: `scripts/build.ts` copies `better-sqlite3`'s prebuilt `better_sqlite3.node` beside `daemon.mjs`, and `src/paths.ts` hands its path to `better-sqlite3` as `nativeBinding`. `@lydell/node-pty` takes no path. It `require`s `@lydell/node-pty-<platform>-<arch>/pty.node` by package name and, on macOS, `require.resolve`s that package's `spawn-helper`. esbuild bundles the fork's JavaScript and leaves those computed requires to the bundle's `createRequire`. So the build copies the one platform package, `package.json`, `pty.node` and `spawn-helper` with its mode, to `node_modules/@lydell/node-pty-<platform>-<arch>/` beside `daemon.mjs` (Task 11). Tauri's resource mapping (`resources/daemon/` → `daemon/`) carries the folder, so `tauri.conf.json` doesn't change. `spawn-helper` is a second Mach-O executable in the app's Resources, beside `better_sqlite3.node`, and with `pty.node` a third native file there. `codesign --verify --deep --strict` treats Mach-O files under `Contents/Resources` as data, so it passes them unsigned, while notarization rejects them and macOS refuses an unsigned, quarantined one on exec. So a signed release signs all three itself, with the hardened runtime, after `pnpm sidecar` and before Tauri signs the app (`scripts/release/sign-native.sh` as Tauri's `beforeBundleCommand`), and its signed branch verifies them in the built app (Task 11). Apple signing isn't set up yet, so the first signed release proves it; Task 16 checks an unsigned build runs the bundled PTY.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-10db-factory-loop-design.md`. This plan implements:

- decision 3 ("Open in terminal" is an explicit, serialized takeover; never driven two ways at once);
- §5.1 `TAKEN_OVER`;
- §5.4 Takeover, steps 1–5 (pause at a safe point, release the lease, `claude --resume <id>` in the PTY owns the session, the builder clicks Hand back, 10db reconciles git, managed mode resumes);
- §10 _Open in terminal_ (from the Factory and the board card) and its xterm.js terminal;
- §13 M2 "takeover".

It amends three spec lines, recorded as decisions (T1, T2):

- §3 "The PTY lives in the Tauri shell": the PTY lives in the daemon.
- §10 "The detail view has … _Open in terminal_": the Factory has no detail view yet, so the Factory row and the board card open the terminal view.
- §9 "SessionStart hook for terminal sessions": a resumed conversation already holds its briefing, so nothing is injected.

It builds on spike 06 (`docs/architecture/research/spike-06-park-resume.md`: a stopped or parked conversation resumes by id with the same session id, and no process survives the stop) and spike 10 (`spike-10-bg-daemon.md`: `claude agents --json` lists interactive sessions). The CLI flags used here (`--resume`, `--model`, `--effort`, `--append-system-prompt`, and `--fork-session` being off by default, so a resume keeps the session id) were read from `claude --help` on Claude Code 2.1.306 on 2026-10-22. Task 16 checks them live.

**Plan series:** `00-spikes` … `07-m2b-board-and-settings`, `08-batch-delivery`, `09-accounts`, **`10-takeover` (this file)**, `11` (M3 knowledge), `12-accounts-settings`. This plan was written against `main` at `2b3d811` with the batch delivery and M2b Part A branches, revised after its plan review against `main` at `a143da3`, and revised again for Travis's decisions against `main` at `bee3f2e` and `origin/docs/accounts-plan` at `86e0e67` (projects move between accounts mid-work; the default account is "Claude plan"), then after its own plan review. Where a file, signature or anchor it names doesn't match the code, trust the code, adapt the step and say so in the task's report. That applies above all to the accounts plan's code (`environment/launch.ts`, `accounts/`, `insertSession`, the test engine), which this plan has read only as a plan.

## Travis's decisions

Travis decided the questions the first draft left open. Each is written into the decision rows below and into the tasks.

| #   | Question                                   | Decided                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Row      |
| --- | ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| 1   | Which terminal                             | **Embedded**: an xterm.js pane inside the 10db window on a PTY the daemon owns, so it works in the Tauri window and the browser dev loop, Playwright covers it, and it survives a closed window. Not an external app. | T1, T5   |
| 2   | What the terminal session runs with        | **The builder's own Claude Code, no harness**: no 10db MCP server, context-pack hook or guard. Its environment comes from the accounts launcher, for the project's current account.                                | T2       |
| 3   | The safe-point wait                        | The next tool call, the end of the turn or a wait on the builder; **60 s**, then interrupt.                                                                                                                          | T3       |
| 4   | The terminal left the batch branch         | **Refuse Hand back** with the fix spelled out.                                                                                                                                                                       | T6       |
| 5   | Lanes and caps                             | A taken-over session holds **no lane** and no lane-cap slot.                                                                                                                                                         | T4       |
| 6   | Taking over a `FAILED` workstream          | **Yes** (rescue): hand back retries it on the same conversation, resetting its failure count as Retry does.                                                                                                          | T12      |
| 7   | When Claude exits in the terminal          | **No card.** The row, the card and the terminal view say how it ended.                                                                                                                                               | T5       |
| 8   | Two takeovers in one project               | **One at a time**; the refusal names the other.                                                                                                                                                                      | T11      |
| 9   | A conversation on an account the project has left | **A fresh conversation on the project's current account**, briefed as a moved lane is (the step's handoff, else "inspect git status first"), with a known id (`--session-id`) that hand-back resumes. Not the old account, and no refusal. | T2 |
| 10  | The subscription start gate                | **Taken** from spawn until Claude first prints or exits, at most 30 s.                                                                                                                                               | T5       |
| 11  | Terminal spend                             | **Not metered for now**; the open items and the monthly-cap card say so.                                                                                                                                             | T2       |
| 12  | A rescued workstream's failure card        | **Stays open**; its Retry says to hand the workstream back.                                                                                                                                                          | T12      |

## Still open for Travis

Nothing blocks this plan. The follow-ups are open items (Task 15): a pop-out to an external terminal app, metering a terminal's spend from its transcript, the first signed release's check of the native files, and carrying an old conversation across accounts instead of starting fresh.

## New decisions

Task 15 appends these to `docs/architecture/decisions.md` as consecutive rows numbered from the next free number at merge. `main` ends at row 88 (batch delivery took 78–86). The accounts plan lands first and takes the next fourteen (AC1–AC14, 89–102 if nothing else lands between), so the takeover rows take 103 onward (T1–T12 as 103–114). Check `main` and renumber. The code follows them from Task 1.

| Label | Decision | Why |
| ----- | -------- | --- |
| T1 | "Open in terminal" opens a terminal inside the 10db window: an xterm.js pane (`@xterm/xterm` with `@xterm/addon-fit`) on the view `/terminal/$workstreamId`, which the Factory row and the board card open. The PTY lives in the daemon (`@lydell/node-pty`, imported only by `terminal/pty.ts`). The pane reaches it over a WebSocket on the daemon's own server, `/workstreams/:id/terminal`, with the event stream's auth: the UI token in the query (a browser can't set a WebSocket header), the allowed-origin check, and the listener on `127.0.0.1` only; the server takes no frame over `TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES`. The pane sends `input` and `resize`; the daemon sends `output` and `exit`. The bundle carries the PTY's platform package, and a signed release signs `pty.node`, `spawn-helper` and `better_sqlite3.node` with the hardened runtime before Tauri signs the app (`sign-native.sh`), then verifies them in the built app; until Apple signing is set up, the first signed release is what proves it. This amends spec §3 ("The PTY lives in the Tauri shell") and §10 (the Factory has no detail view yet, so the row and the card open the terminal), and uses §10's xterm.js. | One pane of glass. A daemon-owned PTY works the same in the Tauri window and the browser dev loop, so Playwright's WebKit covers it end to end, and it outlives a closed window, so the pane reattaches. Tauri 2's PTY plugins are hobby projects (`external-research-recheck.md` §5). One WebSocket carries keystrokes and output in order with the auth the server already has; a POST per keystroke can arrive out of order, and terminal bytes must not enter the persisted event log. `codesign --verify --deep` treats Mach-O files in Resources as data and passes them unsigned; notarization doesn't. |
| T2 | The terminal runs on the project's current account, spawned with no shell, in the repo, in the environment the accounts launcher builds for that account (`Launcher.session`). When the conversation ran on that account, it runs `claude --resume <provider session id> --model <model> --effort <effort> --append-system-prompt <note>`. When it ran on an account the project has since left, the terminal starts a fresh conversation instead, briefed as a moved lane is (accounts AC11): `claude --session-id <new uuid> --model … --effort … --append-system-prompt <note> <briefing>`, where the briefing is the task, its claims and the step's latest handoff as its `continue_from_handoff` prompt (or "inspect git status first" when there is none). The takeover record and the terminal's session row then hold the new id and the current account, the banner says the earlier conversation ran on the old account, and hand-back resumes the new conversation. The note says 10db's MCP tools are unavailable here: commit with plain git, ask the builder directly, write no handoff. It gets no 10db harness, MCP server, context-pack hook or guard (this amends spec §9's `SessionStart` hook for terminal sessions; a resumed conversation already holds its briefing). A takeover is refused (409) before the managed session is touched when that account is archived, unready, paused or at its monthly cap, or its launch fails (a secret missing or changed), and (400) when `claude` is missing or can't be run. On any account but the default, Claude Code runs in that account's own config directory, which starts empty, so the builder's `~/.claude` settings and plugins aren't there. 10db doesn't meter a terminal's spend: account usage and the monthly cap don't count it, and the cap card says so. | Travis's decision. A human drives it, so Claude's own permission prompts are the guard. Claude Code keeps a conversation in its account's config directory, and the project now bills another account, so the old conversation can't continue there; starting fresh from the handoff is what a moved lane does, and a known id lets hand-back resume the terminal's work. The resumed transcript still lists 10db's tools, so without the note its first commit would reach for `commit`. Only the launcher names account variables (the accounts plan's launcher row), and it already refuses a credential the account doesn't hold; a refusal before the pause leaves the session running. |
| T3 | Only `RUNNING`, `HANDING_OFF`, `WAITING_HUMAN` and `FAILED` (see the rescue row) with a started provider session can be taken over (`TAKEABLE_STATES`), and not while the gate runs (`nextAction` `verify` on a live workstream: 409, "take it over after it finishes"). A live session gets a notice and stops at its next tool call (denied before it runs), at the end of its turn or while it waits on the builder. After `takeoverWaitMs` (60 s) it is interrupted. A cancel, a shutdown, a usage limit or a crash during the wait wins, and the takeover answers 409. A takeover with nothing to take over (verification began, or the session stopped before it began a conversation) lapses at once: the step runs on, no recovery attempt is spent, and the takeover answers 409. Taking over cancels the workstream's open question and permission cards in the same write as the transition; the terminal session asks again in its own way. | Spec §5.4 "pause at a safe point". A denied tool call is a point where nothing is half done. The deadline keeps the button from hanging behind a long test run, and refusing during the gate keeps it from hanging behind a 30-minute gate. |
| T4 | A `TAKEN_OVER` workstream keeps its claims but holds no lease, no lane and no account lane-cap slot. Keep-awake (`while_running`) holds while its terminal's process runs. The watcher watches its project, and treats its writes inside its own claims as its own: in real time for as long as it is taken over (the builder may edit by hand after `/exit`, before he hands back), and at any other lane's step end. This amends decision 45's exception for a workstream a human took over. | Other work may run beside a human, as with the builder's other tabs. Claims still keep agents out of the areas he is editing. A long task left running in the terminal must not let the Mac sleep. Flagging a human's own edits as strays is noise that comes back on every step end. |
| T5 | A terminal is a `sessions` row with `mode: terminal`, its provider session id, the account it runs on and an MCP token hash nobody holds; its pid and start time are recorded once it spawns. A resumed conversation's row copies `briefed` and `briefingBaselinePct` from the session it continues; a fresh one is `briefed` when its briefing carries a handoff. On a subscription account a terminal takes the account's start gate (accounts AC8) from spawn until Claude first prints or exits, at most `TERMINAL_START_GATE_MS` (30 s). The daemon owns the process. When Claude exits, the row ends (`terminal_exited`) and the takeover records how (`lastExit`: the exit status, or the signal that ended it); a spawn that throws ends it as `terminal_spawn_failed`, with the error. No card is raised: the Factory row, the board card and the terminal view say how it ended. Closing the pane or the window ends nothing. A closed terminal is no longer the workstream's current session and can be opened again, which spawns `claude` on the same conversation. Its output lives only in memory: the daemon keeps each terminal's newest `scrollbackChars` (262 144) for a pane that attaches or reattaches, and no byte of it reaches disk, a log, an event or the database. | node-pty reports the exit itself, so nothing polls a pid. A pane can come and go; the process is the session. A terminal never reports `started`, so its first output stands for it in the start gate, with a deadline for one that prints nothing. The builder is at the terminal when it exits. A terminal can print anything the builder types or Claude reads, secrets included. |
| T6 | Hand back is refused while the terminal's process runs, checked before git is read and again inside the hand-back transaction. It is also refused when the checkout is detached, on a branch other than the batch branch (for work in an unfinished batch) or the takeover branch, or when history no longer contains the takeover `HEAD`; each refusal says how to fix it. Otherwise, in that transaction: the credited commits and a `recovery.step` (`handed_back`) are recorded, and the workstream goes `TAKEN_OVER → RECOVERING → QUEUED` with `resume` on the same conversation and a reason that names the terminal work. Hand-back ends no session, because none is open by then, and drops the terminal's scrollback. | Spec §5.4 "10db reconciles git state". A refusal can be fixed in place and loses nothing; failing the batch would add a second thing to undo. Resuming the same conversation hands the managed session everything the builder did. The second check closes the gap in which Open terminal again could land while git was read. |
| T7 | A terminal session is not guarded. The builder (or Claude, with his approval) commits with plain git, authored by the repo's git config. At hand-back, 10db credits to the workstream every commit since the takeover `HEAD` that no other workstream recorded through `commit` after the takeover, as `commit.recorded` with `via: 'terminal'`. Their paths and the dirty paths go through the watcher's step-end claim check: strays become flag cards, never refusals. | Spec §8's guard is for agents; a human at a terminal is trusted like any git user. Lanes beside him keep committing through `commit`, so their commits are already attributed and must not be credited twice. With one takeover per project (T11), every other commit is his. |
| T8 | Cancelling a `TAKEN_OVER` workstream is refused while its terminal's process runs, checked again inside the cancelling transaction. Otherwise, in that transaction, it cancels the workstream's open cards, cancels the workstream and releases its claims, and the terminal's scrollback is dropped. | Releasing claims under a live terminal would let agents into the files the builder is editing. |
| T9 | Takeover is refused while the workstream's batch is `CUTTING` or `SHIPPING`. A `TAKEN_OVER` member holds its batch `OPEN` and refuses Ship anyway and Abandon. A batch cut, which switches branches, is refused with a card naming the workstream while any workstream in the project is `TAKEN_OVER`, one outside every batch included (say one taken over before the project's delivery became pull requests). A restart while the checkout is on another branch fails the batch (batch delivery decision B8); the builder switches back, hands back, and presses Retry, which reopens the batch because its work is unfinished. | Nothing in a batch may change under its cut or its shipper, and the cut's lane count doesn't see a taken-over workstream, which holds no lane. A taken-over member is unfinished work. The batch plan's startup rule stays as it is. |
| T10 | A daemon shutdown refuses new terminals and ends each running one: SIGHUP to the program, then, after `stopGraceMs` (3 s), SIGKILL to its process group, which reaches the children it started (forkpty makes it a session leader); its session row ends `daemon_shutdown`. At startup, recovery terminates a terminal process that outlived a crash (matched by pid and start time, as for managed sessions) and ends its row `daemon_restart`. Either way the workstream stays `TAKEN_OVER` with its record and its claims, `lastExit` says the daemon stopped, and **Open terminal again** resumes the conversation. A known limit: the pid is recorded after spawn, once `ps` answers, so a crash in between leaves recovery nothing to match; that process got the PTY's hangup when the daemon died, and one that ignored it runs on until the builder ends it. | The daemon holds the PTY's master, the only way to type into the process; a process whose master is gone can't be driven and would hold the conversation as an orphan. Claude Code writes its transcript as it goes, so a resume loses no finished turn. |
| T11 | One workstream per project can be taken over at a time. A takeover is refused (409, naming the other workstream) while another workstream in the same project is `TAKEN_OVER` or is being taken over; opening one's own closed terminal again is not affected. | Two terminals in one checkout are unsafe anyway: they edit the same files with no claims between them. With one takeover per project, every terminal commit since the takeover is that workstream's, so commit credit (T7) is unambiguous. |
| T12 | A `FAILED` workstream with a started provider session can be taken over (`FAILED → TAKEN_OVER`, a rescue) when Retry would accept it: its task isn't parked, it is still the task's latest workstream, and its batch, if any, is `OPEN` or `FAILED` (a `CUTTING` or `SHIPPING` batch refuses as in T9, a finished one as Retry does). The claims it released when it failed are taken back in the takeover's write; a claim another workstream holds now refuses the rescue, naming the holder. The record marks it `rescued`. Hand back then does what Retry does, in the same write: it resets `attempt`, `recoveryAttempts` and `failureReason`, restarts the current step clean, reopens a `FAILED` batch (`FAILED → OPEN`) and queues `resume` on the same conversation. Cancel works as for any taken-over workstream. In a batch the rescued member is unfinished work: it holds an `OPEN` batch open, and Ship anyway (strip or card) is refused with "still has unfinished work"; Abandon is refused while it is taken over; a `FAILED` batch stays `FAILED` until hand-back reopens it, unless the strip's Retry reopens it first (decision 81: unfinished work reopens, never ships). The failed member's card stays open, as after a Retry from the strip (Travis's decision): its Ship anyway is refused while the member is taken over, and its Retry answers that hand-back is the retry (`retryWorkstream`'s `TAKEN_OVER` arm). Takeover and hand-back both re-check Retry's rules inside their writes. | Failed work is often a step from done, and the conversation that failed holds the context to finish it. Retry's resets keep a rescued workstream from failing again at its next crash. The claims keep agents out of the files the builder edits. A batch never ships work a human is still finishing. |

## Global Constraints

Everything in M0's, M1a's, M1b's, M2a's, the batch delivery plan's and the accounts plan's Global Constraints still applies. On top of that:

- **Branch:** `feat/takeover`, cut from `main` once the accounts plan (Tasks 1–12) is merged. Open one PR to `main` at the end; its title is a Conventional Commit.
- **Commits:**
  - Each commit is one command that names its paths: `git add <path> … && git commit -m '…'`. Never `git add -A`, `git add .` or `git commit -a`.
  - The scope is one of `daemon`, `desktop`, `contracts`, `db`, `harness`, `repo`, `ci`, `docs`, `deps`. The subject is lowercase and the header is at most 100 characters. Use single quotes for `-m`.
  - Every message ends with the attribution footer your own session's instructions give (for example a `Co-Authored-By:` line and a session link), after a blank line, one extra `-m` paragraph per line. The commit commands below write it as `-m '<attribution>'`: replace that with one `-m` per footer line, never both lines in one `-m`. Never copy another session's link.
  - Run `pnpm format` first. A failing hook leaves the paths staged: fix the cause and rerun the same line. Never `--no-verify`, `LEFTHOOK=0` or `LEFTHOOK_EXCLUDE`.
- **Dependencies** are pinned exactly and added with `pnpm add --save-exact`: `@lydell/node-pty@1.1.0` in `apps/daemon` (Task 4), `@xterm/xterm@6.0.0` and `@xterm/addon-fit@0.11.0` in `apps/desktop` (Task 12). If a newer stable release exists when a task runs, take it and say so in the task's report; a held-back pin gets a `decisions.md` row. `@lydell/node-pty` has no install script, so `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `allowBuilds` doesn't change.
- **Generated files** are regenerated, never edited: `pnpm --filter @10db/contracts generate` after a contracts change. Task 2's schema change regenerates the migration baseline (`packages/db/AGENTS.md`); there is still no published release, so there is no new migration file. After Task 2, delete any local `.dev/data`.
- **Contracts are additive** (`packages/contracts/AGENTS.md`): new schemas, a new event type, three new workflow edges and a new nullable `WorkstreamDto.takeover`. `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 3.
- **Terminal output stays in memory.** No byte a terminal prints or receives is written to disk, a log line, an event or the database; logs name the workstream and the session, never the text. The scrollback is bounded by `scrollbackChars`.
- **Only the accounts launcher builds the terminal's environment.** No module in this plan names an account or billing variable; the takeover service adds only `TERM` and `COLORTERM` to `SessionLaunch.env`.
- **The terminal channel has the event stream's auth and nothing more permissive:** the UI token from the `token` query parameter, the allowed-origin check on the upgrade, the `127.0.0.1` listener. A Bearer header alone does not open it. The WebSocket server's `maxPayload` is `TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES`, not ws's 100 MiB default, so an oversized frame closes the socket before it is parsed. Every message from the pane is parsed with `TerminalClientMessage` and a bad one closes the socket.
- **A terminal's process belongs to the daemon:** closing a pane or the window never ends it; `/exit`, a shutdown or a restart does (T5, T10).
- **The child process is the test boundary.** Tests run the real `@lydell/node-pty` with a fake `claude`, a POSIX `sh` script written by `test/fake-claude.ts` (daemon tests) or `e2e/prepare.ts` (Playwright), as `.claude/rules/testing.md` allows for a child process 10db doesn't own. Only the spawn-failure cases (`terminal/host.test.ts`, and one in `takeover/service.test.ts`) replace the PTY spawner itself. `scripts/release/sign-native.sh` is tested with a stub `codesign` on `PATH`, for the same reason.
- **No `as` casts, no `!`, no `any`** except at a validated boundary with a comment (`.claude/rules/code-style.md`). Exhaustive switches over `TerminalStatus`, `TerminalEndReason`, `StopReason`, `TerminalClientMessage['type']` and `TerminalServerMessage['type']`.
- **Every write that acts on a check re-reads inside its transaction.** Hand-back awaits git, so another request can land in between. Opening a terminal, hand-back and cancel each re-read the workstream inside their `state.write` and refuse unless it is still `TAKEN_OVER` with no terminal running; the parked and the rescued takeover re-read `WAITING_HUMAN` or `FAILED` in their write, and the rescue and its hand-back re-check Retry's rules (`retryRefusal`) there too.
- **Tests** (`.claude/rules/testing.md`): red first, with the expected failure written in each step; real SQLite, real git repos and real child processes; table-driven for the refusal rules and the channel's messages; no sleeps (wait on `waitFor`, `waitForAsync`, `runner.idle()` or a terminal's output with a deadline; the `ManualClock` drives the safe-point wait and the shutdown grace). A test that pins behavior an earlier plan built proves it can fail by a stated, reverted mutation.
- **Tunable values are named constants:** `DEFAULT_RUNNER_LIMITS.takeoverWaitMs = 60_000`, `DEFAULT_TERMINAL_LIMITS = { scrollbackChars: 262_144, stopGraceMs: 3_000 }`, `DEFAULT_TERMINAL_SIZE = { cols: 120, rows: 32 }`, `TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS = { minCols: 2, maxCols: 1_000, minRows: 2, maxRows: 500 }`, `TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX = 65_536`, `TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES = TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX * 6 + 1_024`, `TerminalCloseCode = { BadMessage: 4400, NoTerminal: 4404 }`, `TERMINAL_START_GATE_MS = 30_000`, `MAX_LISTED_FILES = 200`.
- **knip:** an `index.ts` re-exports only what another module or a test imports.
- **Comments never carry decision numbers** (`.claude/rules/code-style.md`); they cite the spec (`spec §5.4`) or say the why in words.

## Review Focus

The conditions most likely to bite a person, each with the task whose tests pin it:

1. **Two drivers at once.**
   - Case: the builder presses Hand back, Cancel or Open terminal again while Claude still runs in the terminal. Or two requests race: Open terminal again lands while Hand back reads git. Or he resumes the conversation in a second tab of his own. Or he takes a second workstream over in the same project. Or two 10db windows show the same terminal.
   - Hand back, Cancel and Open again are refused while the terminal's process runs, with the pid and "quit it with /exit". Each re-reads the workstream inside its write, so the request that writes second is refused and the terminal that opened stays the only driver. A second takeover in the project is refused with the first one's name (T11). Two panes on one terminal are one driver: both see the output and both type into the same process. A tab the builder opens himself is outside 10db's sight; the banner and README say so.
   - Tests: Task 6 (`refuses to open it again while Claude runs in the terminal`, `refuses a second takeover in the same project, naming the first`), Task 4 (`streams one terminal to two panes, and either types into it`), Task 8 (`refuses while Claude runs in the terminal, then resumes the same conversation…`, `is refused while Claude runs in the terminal, then cancels…`, `refuses to hand back once the terminal was opened again meanwhile…`).
2. **The terminal moved the checkout.**
   - Case: `git switch -c side`, `git checkout --detach`, `git reset --hard` or a rebase below the takeover point, or (in a batch) a takeover of a parked workstream while the checkout was already off the batch branch.
   - Hand back is refused with the fix spelled out; the workstream stays `TAKEN_OVER` and nothing is lost. In a batch, the batch branch is what counts.
   - Tests: Task 8 (`handBackRefusal` table and `refuses to hand back after the terminal …` rows), Task 9 (`refuses hand back off the batch branch…`).
3. **The session never reaches a safe point, or something else happens first.**
   - Case: a tool that runs for 20 minutes; a question waiting on a card; a turn that ends; a cancel or a pause during the wait; a takeover asked for while the runner already stops for another reason; a takeover asked for while the gate runs, or before the session has begun a conversation.
   - Each ends in a defined state: `TAKEN_OVER` after at most `takeoverWaitMs`, or the other outcome wins and the takeover answers 409 naming the state. During the gate the request is refused at once, and a takeover with nothing to take over lapses at once; neither waits on the gate or spends a recovery attempt.
   - Tests: Task 5 (`takeover` describe, eight cases), Task 6 (`answers 409 when the session ends some other way first`, the `about to run its gate` refusal row).
4. **A restart or Quit while taken over.**
   - Case: `kill -9` of the daemon, Quit from the menu bar, or a reboot while Claude runs in the pane; the window closed and reopened.
   - A closed window ends nothing, and the pane reattaches with the recent output. A shutdown or restart ends the terminal and records why; the workstream stays `TAKEN_OVER` with its claims, and Open terminal again resumes the conversation. If the checkout was off the batch branch at a restart, the batch fails at startup and the way back works.
   - Tests: Task 4 (`ends every terminal at stopAll: SIGHUP, then SIGKILL after the grace`, which also checks a child the program started is gone and that no terminal opens once shutdown began), Task 6 (`keeps Claude running when its pane detaches…`), Task 9 (`a shutdown` › `ends the terminal, keeps the takeover, and opens it again on the same conversation`, `ends a terminal that outlived a crash, and keeps the workstream taken over`, `restarts with the checkout off the batch branch…`), Task 11 (`takes a session over, drives its terminal over the WebSocket…`).
5. **The wrong account, or the wrong billing, reaches the terminal.**
   - Case: the builder's login shell exports `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK` or a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; the project's account is paused, unready or at its cap; the project moved to another account after the conversation began.
   - The terminal's env is the accounts launcher's for the project's account, nothing from the daemon's or the login shell's billing; an account that can't start a session refuses the takeover before the managed session stops; a moved project's terminal starts a fresh conversation on its new account, briefed from the handoff, and the banner says so; a subscription terminal starts one at a time with the account's other sessions.
   - Tests: Task 6 (`runs claude in the launcher's environment for the conversation's account…`, the `refuses a takeover the terminal could not run…` rows, `starts a fresh conversation on the project's account, briefed from the step's handoff…`, the two start-gate cases).
6. **A keystroke path into a shell without the token, or from another origin.**
   - Case: a page on another origin opens `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>/workstreams/<id>/terminal`; a client presents only a Bearer header, a wrong token, or none; a client sends a malformed or oversized message, or names a workstream with no terminal.
   - Every upgrade without the UI token in the query answers 401, a foreign origin 403, as on `/events/stream`; a bad message closes the socket (4400); a frame past `TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES` closes it (1009) before parsing; a workstream with no terminal closes it (4404).
   - Tests: Task 11 (`takes the token from the query string on the terminal channel too`, `refuses a terminal upgrade from a foreign origin`, the `terminalEvents` table, and the daemon test's refusal without the token and its oversized frame).
7. **Commits credited to the wrong work.**
   - Case: another lane commits through `commit` while the builder works in the terminal; the builder commits a file outside the task's claims; a second terminal in the same project commits too.
   - Only the commits no other workstream recorded are credited; a stray path gets a flag card; nothing is refused. A second takeover in the project is refused (T11), so no other terminal's commits are there to credit.
   - Tests: Task 8 (`credits only the commits no other workstream recorded`, `flags a terminal commit outside the claims, and hands back anyway`), Task 6 (`refuses a second takeover in the same project, naming the first`).
8. **The terminal never starts, or Claude quits at once.**
   - Case: `claude` missing or not executable, the PTY addon failing to spawn, or `claude --resume` printing "No conversation found" and exiting.
   - A missing or unrunnable `claude` refuses the takeover before the session stops. A spawn that throws leaves the takeover standing with the error in the row and Open terminal again available. A quick exit shows Claude's own output and the exit status in the pane.
   - Tests: Task 6 (`refuses a takeover the terminal could not run…` rows, `records why the terminal could not start, and opens it again on request`, `marks the terminal closed when Claude exits…`), Task 4 (`reports how the program ended…`).
9. **The resumed conversation reaches for 10db's tools.**
   - Case: the transcript the terminal resumes lists `mcp__10db__commit`, `write_handoff` and `ask_human`, which the terminal doesn't have.
   - The terminal appends a system prompt that says so: commit with plain git, ask the builder directly, write no handoff.
   - Tests: Task 6 (`pauses a running session and opens its conversation in a terminal the daemon owns`); Task 16 checks it live.
10. **A rescue in a batch.**
    - Case: the builder rescues a failed member of an `OPEN` batch and then presses Ship anyway; rescues a member of a `FAILED` batch; rescues one whose batch already shipped; rescues one whose area another lane holds now.
    - Ship anyway and Abandon are refused while it is taken over; hand back reopens a `FAILED` batch; a finished batch and a held claim refuse the rescue with the reason.
    - Tests: Task 10 (`rescuing a failed member` › `of an open batch…`, `of a failed batch…`, `of a batch that shipped…`; `rescuing a failed workstream` › `refuses when $name` rows).
11. **A terminal floods its output.**
    - Case: a test run prints megabytes into the pane, or a pane attaches an hour after Claude started.
    - The daemon keeps only the newest `scrollbackChars`; a late pane gets that, then live output; memory stays bounded.
    - Tests: Task 4 (`keeps only the newest output for a pane that attaches late`).

### Task 1: Contracts for the takeover record, the terminal channel and the takeover edges

New schemas and vocabularies plus three workflow edges. `WorkstreamDto` gains its field in Task 2, together with the column it is read from.

**Files:**

- Create: `packages/contracts/src/takeover.ts`
- Modify: `packages/contracts/src/workflow.ts`, `events.ts`, `index.ts`, `json-schema.ts`
- Test: `packages/contracts/src/takeover.test.ts` (new), `packages/contracts/src/workflow.test.ts`
- Generated: `packages/contracts/generated/takeover-dto.schema.json`, `terminal-client-message.schema.json`, `terminal-server-message.schema.json`, `daemon-event.schema.json`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `WorkstreamState`, `canTransition` (`states.ts`, `workflow.ts`), `SessionId` and the accounts plan's `AccountId` (`ids.ts`).
- Produces (used by every later task):
  - `TerminalStatus` (`open | closed`).
  - `TerminalEndReason` (`exited | spawn_failed | daemon_shutdown | daemon_restart`) and `TerminalExit`: `{ reason: TerminalEndReason; exitCode: number | null; signal: number | null; message: string | null }`.
  - `TAKEABLE_STATES: readonly WorkstreamState[]` (`RUNNING`, `HANDING_OFF`, `WAITING_HUMAN`, `FAILED`).
  - `TakeoverRecord`: `{ providerSessionId: string; accountId: AccountId; head: string (40 hex); branch: string | null; takenAt: string; eventSeq: number; rescued: boolean; freshFrom: AccountId | null; lastExit: TerminalExit | null }`.
  - `TakeoverDto`: `TakeoverRecord & { terminal: TerminalStatus; terminalSessionId: SessionId | null; terminalPid: number | null }`.
  - `TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS`, `TerminalSize` (`{ cols: number; rows: number }` within the limits), `TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX = 65_536`, `TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES` (the WebSocket's `maxPayload`), `TerminalCloseCode` (`BadMessage: 4400`, `NoTerminal: 4404`).
  - `TerminalClientMessage`: `{ type: 'input'; data: string }` (1 to `TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX` characters) or `{ type: 'resize'; cols; rows }`.
  - `TerminalServerMessage`: `{ type: 'output'; data: string }` or `{ type: 'exit'; exit: TerminalExit }`.
  - `EventType.TakeoverChanged = 'takeover.changed'` (`entityType: 'workstream'`).
  - New edges `HANDING_OFF → TAKEN_OVER`, `WAITING_HUMAN → TAKEN_OVER` and `FAILED → TAKEN_OVER`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Check that this plan's foundations are on `main`**

Run:

```bash
git fetch origin && git switch -c feat/takeover origin/main && test -f apps/daemon/src/delivery/index.ts && test -f apps/desktop/src/views/board/task-card-menu.tsx && test -f apps/daemon/src/environment/launch.ts && test -f apps/daemon/src/accounts/index.ts && grep -q 'accountId' packages/db/src/store/sessions.ts && grep -q 'idle()' apps/daemon/src/scheduler/scheduler.ts && echo 'foundations present'
```

Expected: `foundations present`. If anything is missing, stop: the accounts plan (09), batch delivery (08) or M2b Part A (07) is not merged yet.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

`packages/contracts/src/takeover.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { canTransition, mintId, WorkstreamState as S } from './index';
import {
  TAKEABLE_STATES,
  TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX,
  TerminalClientMessage,
  TerminalServerMessage,
  TakeoverDto,
  TakeoverRecord,
} from './takeover';

const record = {
  providerSessionId: '4464fa82-0c1d-4c8e-9f8a-2b3c4d5e6f70',
  accountId: mintId('Account'),
  head: 'a'.repeat(40),
  branch: 'main',
  takenAt: '2026-10-22T12:00:00.000Z',
  eventSeq: 41,
  rescued: false,
  freshFrom: null,
  lastExit: null,
};

describe('TakeoverRecord', () => {
  it.each([
    { name: 'a takeover on a branch', value: record, ok: true },
    { name: 'a takeover on a detached HEAD', value: { ...record, branch: null }, ok: true },
    { name: 'a rescue of failed work', value: { ...record, rescued: true }, ok: true },
    {
      name: 'a fresh conversation after the project left the account it ran on',
      value: { ...record, freshFrom: mintId('Account') },
      ok: true,
    },
    {
      name: 'a terminal that exited',
      value: { ...record, lastExit: { reason: 'exited', exitCode: 0, signal: null, message: null } },
      ok: true,
    },
    {
      name: 'a terminal ended by a signal',
      value: { ...record, lastExit: { reason: 'exited', exitCode: null, signal: 9, message: null } },
      ok: true,
    },
    {
      name: 'a terminal that could not spawn',
      value: {
        ...record,
        lastExit: {
          reason: 'spawn_failed',
          exitCode: null,
          signal: null,
          message: 'posix_spawnp failed.',
        },
      },
      ok: true,
    },
    {
      name: 'an unknown end',
      value: {
        ...record,
        lastExit: { reason: 'vanished', exitCode: null, signal: null, message: null },
      },
      ok: false,
    },
    { name: 'an abbreviated HEAD', value: { ...record, head: 'abc1234' }, ok: false },
    { name: 'no provider session', value: { ...record, providerSessionId: '' }, ok: false },
    { name: 'no account', value: { ...record, accountId: 'Claude plan' }, ok: false },
    { name: 'an empty branch name', value: { ...record, branch: '' }, ok: false },
    { name: 'a negative event seq', value: { ...record, eventSeq: -1 }, ok: false },
  ])('$name', ({ value, ok }) => {
    expect(TakeoverRecord.safeParse(value).success).toBe(ok);
  });

  it('describes the terminal beside the record', () => {
    const open = { ...record, terminal: 'open', terminalSessionId: null, terminalPid: 4242 };
    expect(TakeoverDto.parse(open).terminal).toBe('open');
    expect(TakeoverDto.safeParse({ ...open, terminal: 'launching' }).success).toBe(false);
    expect(TakeoverDto.safeParse({ ...open, terminalPid: 0 }).success).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('TAKEABLE_STATES', () => {
  it('are exactly the states with an edge to TAKEN_OVER', () => {
    const withEdge = Object.values(S).filter((s) => canTransition(s, S.TakenOver));
    expect([...withEdge].sort()).toEqual([...TAKEABLE_STATES].sort());
  });
});

describe('the terminal channel', () => {
  it.each([
    { name: 'a keystroke', value: { type: 'input', data: 'a' }, ok: true },
    { name: 'a paste at the limit', value: { type: 'input', data: 'x'.repeat(TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX) }, ok: true },
    { name: 'an empty input', value: { type: 'input', data: '' }, ok: false },
    {
      name: 'a paste over the limit',
      value: { type: 'input', data: 'x'.repeat(TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX + 1) },
      ok: false,
    },
    { name: 'a resize', value: { type: 'resize', cols: 120, rows: 32 }, ok: true },
    { name: 'a resize to nothing', value: { type: 'resize', cols: 0, rows: 32 }, ok: false },
    { name: 'a resize past the limit', value: { type: 'resize', cols: 120, rows: 501 }, ok: false },
    { name: 'a fractional size', value: { type: 'resize', cols: 80.5, rows: 24 }, ok: false },
    { name: 'an unknown message', value: { type: 'kill' }, ok: false },
  ])('the pane sends $name', ({ value, ok }) => {
    expect(TerminalClientMessage.safeParse(value).success).toBe(ok);
  });

  it.each([
    { name: 'output', value: { type: 'output', data: '\u001b[1mhi\u001b[0m' }, ok: true },
    {
      name: 'an exit',
      value: { type: 'exit', exit: { reason: 'exited', exitCode: 1, signal: null, message: null } },
      ok: true,
    },
    { name: 'an exit with no reason', value: { type: 'exit', exit: { exitCode: 1 } }, ok: false },
  ])('the daemon sends $name', ({ value, ok }) => {
    expect(TerminalServerMessage.safeParse(value).success).toBe(ok);
  });
});
```

In `packages/contracts/src/workflow.test.ts`, add to the `allows %s -> %s` table:

```ts
    [S.Running, S.TakenOver],
    [S.HandingOff, S.TakenOver],
    [S.WaitingHuman, S.TakenOver],
    [S.Failed, S.TakenOver],
    [S.TakenOver, S.Recovering],
    [S.TakenOver, S.Cancelled],
```

and to the `forbids %s -> %s` table:

```ts
    [S.Queued, S.TakenOver],
    [S.Starting, S.TakenOver],
    [S.Verifying, S.TakenOver],
    [S.PausedLimit, S.TakenOver],
    [S.Done, S.TakenOver],
    [S.Cancelled, S.TakenOver],
    [S.TakenOver, S.Running],
```

`packages/contracts/src/takeover.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { AccountId, SessionId } from './ids';
import { WorkstreamState } from './states';

/** A taken-over conversation's terminal: Claude running in the daemon's PTY, or not. */
export const TerminalStatus = { Open: 'open', Closed: 'closed' } as const;
export type TerminalStatus = (typeof TerminalStatus)[keyof typeof TerminalStatus];

/** How a terminal ended: Claude exited, the PTY could not spawn it, or the daemon stopped or restarted. */
export const TerminalEndReason = {
  Exited: 'exited',
  SpawnFailed: 'spawn_failed',
  DaemonShutdown: 'daemon_shutdown',
  DaemonRestart: 'daemon_restart',
} as const;
export type TerminalEndReason = (typeof TerminalEndReason)[keyof typeof TerminalEndReason];

export const TerminalExit = z.object({
  reason: z.enum(TerminalEndReason),
  /** Claude's exit status; null when a signal ended it or it never ran. */
  exitCode: z.int().nullable(),
  /** The signal that ended it; null when it exited or never ran. */
  signal: z.int().nullable(),
  /** Why the PTY could not spawn it; null otherwise. */
  message: z.string().nullable(),
});
export type TerminalExit = z.infer<typeof TerminalExit>;

/**
 * States a builder can take over from (spec §5.4): a session is live or parked on a card, or the work
 * failed and the builder rescues it in a terminal.
 */
export const TAKEABLE_STATES: readonly WorkstreamState[] = [
  WorkstreamState.Running,
  WorkstreamState.HandingOff,
  WorkstreamState.WaitingHuman,
  WorkstreamState.Failed,
];

const Sha = z.string().regex(/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/, 'a full commit SHA');

/** Stored on the workstream while a terminal owns its conversation; hand-back reconciles git against it. */
export const TakeoverRecord = z.object({
  /** The conversation the terminal resumes, and managed mode resumes again after hand-back. */
  providerSessionId: z.string().min(1),
  /** The account the conversation ran on: its config directory holds the transcript the terminal resumes. */
  accountId: AccountId,
  /** HEAD when the managed session stopped. */
  head: Sha,
  /** The checked-out branch when the managed session stopped; null on a detached HEAD. */
  branch: z.string().min(1).nullable(),
  takenAt: z.iso.datetime(),
  /** The newest event seq at takeover: commits other workstreams record after it are theirs. */
  eventSeq: z.int().min(0),
  /** Taken over from FAILED: hand-back resets the failure count and reopens a failed batch, as Retry does. */
  rescued: z.boolean(),
  /**
   * Set while the terminal starts a fresh conversation (`providerSessionId`, on the project's
   * account) because the one it would resume ran on this account, which the project has left;
   * cleared once a terminal on the fresh one has spawned. The banner names it.
   */
  freshFrom: AccountId.nullable(),
  /** How the newest terminal ended; null while it runs and before the first one opens. */
  lastExit: TerminalExit.nullable(),
});
export type TakeoverRecord = z.infer<typeof TakeoverRecord>;

export const TakeoverDto = TakeoverRecord.extend({
  terminal: z.enum(TerminalStatus),
  /** The terminal session while Claude runs in it. */
  terminalSessionId: SessionId.nullable(),
  /** The terminal's process while it runs, once 10db has read its start time. */
  terminalPid: z.int().positive().nullable(),
});
export type TakeoverDto = z.infer<typeof TakeoverDto>;

/** Sizes the PTY accepts; the pane's fit stays within them. */
export const TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS = { minCols: 2, maxCols: 1_000, minRows: 2, maxRows: 500 } as const;

export const TerminalSize = z.object({
  cols: z.int().min(TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS.minCols).max(TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS.maxCols),
  rows: z.int().min(TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS.minRows).max(TERMINAL_SIZE_LIMITS.maxRows),
});
export type TerminalSize = z.infer<typeof TerminalSize>;

/** The most text one input message carries; the pane sends a larger paste in several. */
export const TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX = 65_536;

/**
 * The largest frame the daemon's WebSocket server accepts. An input message is at most
 * TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX UTF-16 units, which JSON can write as up to six bytes each (a control
 * character becomes \u00XX); the rest is the envelope. Far below ws's 100 MiB default.
 */
export const TERMINAL_FRAME_MAX_BYTES = TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX * 6 + 1_024;

/** Why the daemon closes a terminal socket: a message it could not read, or no terminal to attach to. */
export const TerminalCloseCode = { BadMessage: 4400, NoTerminal: 4404 } as const;
export type TerminalCloseCode = (typeof TerminalCloseCode)[keyof typeof TerminalCloseCode];

/** What the pane sends over `/workstreams/:id/terminal`: keystrokes and its size. */
export const TerminalClientMessage = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
  z.object({ type: z.literal('input'), data: z.string().min(1).max(TERMINAL_INPUT_MAX) }),
  TerminalSize.extend({ type: z.literal('resize') }),
]);
export type TerminalClientMessage = z.infer<typeof TerminalClientMessage>;

/** What the daemon sends: the scrollback, then live output, and how the process ended. */
export const TerminalServerMessage = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
  z.object({ type: z.literal('output'), data: z.string() }),
  z.object({ type: z.literal('exit'), exit: TerminalExit }),
]);
export type TerminalServerMessage = z.infer<typeof TerminalServerMessage>;
```

In `packages/contracts/src/events.ts`, add after `StepChanged`:

```ts
  TakeoverChanged: 'takeover.changed',
```

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: Task 1's `TakeoverRecord`, `TakeoverDto`, `TerminalStatus`; the existing `transitionWorkstream`, `clearLease`, `markSessionStarted`, `endSession`; the accounts plan's `insertSession` (which records the account a session launches on: the `accountId` it is given, else the project's), `seedAccount` and `AccountId`.
- Produces:

  - `enterTakeover(ctx: WriteContext, id: WorkstreamId, fence: number | null, record: TakeoverRecord): WorkstreamDto`.   - `setSessionProcess(ctx: WriteContext, id: SessionId, pid: number, pidStart: string): void`.
  - `latestConversation(db: DbReader, workstreamId: WorkstreamId): { providerSessionId: string; accountId: AccountId } | null`: the newest session that started a conversation, and the account it ran on.
  - `commitShasRecordedAfter(db: DbReader, afterSeq: number, except: WorkstreamId): Set<string>`.

```ts
import {
  EventType,
  mintId,
  type AccountId,
  type ProjectDto,
  type TakeoverRecord,
  type WorkstreamId,
} from '@10db/contracts';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  acquireLease,
  commitShasRecordedAfter,
  endSession,
  enterTakeover,
  getWorkstream,
  getWorkstreamRow,
  insertSession,
  insertTask,
  insertWorkstream,
  latestConversation,
  latestEventSeq,
  listSessions,
  listWorkstreams,
  markSessionStarted,
  patchWorkstream,
  setSessionProcess,
  transitionWorkstream,
  type Store,
} from './index';
import { createTestStore, seedAccount, seedProject } from './test-support';

let store: Store;
let close: () => void;
let project: ProjectDto;
beforeEach(() => {
  ({ store, close } = createTestStore());
  project = seedProject(store);
});
afterEach(() => {
  close();
});

function running(title = 'Alpha'): WorkstreamId {
  const task = store.write((ctx) =>
    insertTask(ctx, project.id, { title, acceptanceCriteria: ['it works'], areas: ['api'] }),
  ).value;
  return store.write((ctx) => {
    const ws = insertWorkstream(ctx, task.id);
    transitionWorkstream(ctx, ws.id, { to: 'STARTING', fence: null });
    transitionWorkstream(ctx, ws.id, { to: 'RUNNING', fence: null });
    acquireLease(ctx, ws.id, 'daemon-1', '2999-01-01T00:00:00.000Z');
    return ws.id;
  }).value;
}

const record = (overrides: Partial<TakeoverRecord> = {}): TakeoverRecord => ({
  providerSessionId: 'provider-1',
  accountId: project.accountId,
  head: 'a'.repeat(40),
  branch: 'main',
  takenAt: '2026-10-22T12:00:00.000Z',
  eventSeq: 0,
  rescued: false,
  freshFrom: null,
  lastExit: null,
  ...overrides,
});

function session(
  id: WorkstreamId,
  mode: 'managed' | 'terminal',
  provider: string | null,
  accountId?: AccountId,
) {
  return store.write((ctx) => {
    const s = insertSession(ctx, {
      workstreamId: id,
      provider: 'claude',
      model: 'sonnet',
      effort: 'medium',
      mode,
      mcpTokenHash: mintId('Session'),
      ...(accountId ? { accountId } : {}),
    });
    if (provider) markSessionStarted(ctx, s.id, provider, null);
    if (mode === 'terminal') patchWorkstream(ctx, id, null, { currentSessionId: s.id });
    return s;
  }).value;
}

describe('takeover rows', () => {
  it('enters TAKEN_OVER with the record, and clears the lease and the current session', () => {
    const id = running();
    store.write((ctx) => enterTakeover(ctx, id, null, record()));
    expect(getWorkstreamRow(store.db, id)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'TAKEN_OVER',
      leaseOwner: null,
      currentSessionId: null,
    });
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, id).takeover).toEqual({
      ...record(),
      terminal: 'closed',
      terminalSessionId: null,
      terminalPid: null,
    });
  });

  it('refuses a workstream that is not running, waiting or failed', () => {
    const id = running();
    store.write((ctx) => transitionWorkstream(ctx, id, { to: 'VERIFYING', fence: null }));
    expect(() => store.write((ctx) => enterTakeover(ctx, id, null, record()))).toThrow(
      'Cannot move workstream from VERIFYING to TAKEN_OVER',
    );
  });

  it('takes over a failed workstream, marked as a rescue', () => {
    const id = running();
    store.write((ctx) => transitionWorkstream(ctx, id, { to: 'FAILED', fence: null }));
    store.write((ctx) => enterTakeover(ctx, id, null, record({ rescued: true })));
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, id)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'TAKEN_OVER',
      takeover: { rescued: true, terminal: 'closed' },
    });
  });

  it('reads its terminal as open while its session runs, with the pid once recorded, then closed', () => {
    const id = running();
    store.write((ctx) => enterTakeover(ctx, id, null, record()));
    const terminal = session(id, 'terminal', 'provider-1');
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, id).takeover).toMatchObject({
      terminal: 'open',
      terminalSessionId: terminal.id,
      terminalPid: null,
    });
    store.write((ctx) => {
      setSessionProcess(ctx, terminal.id, 4242, 'Wed Oct 22 12:00:00 2026');
    });
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, id).takeover).toMatchObject({
      terminal: 'open',
      terminalPid: 4242,
    });
    store.write((ctx) => {
      endSession(ctx, terminal.id, 'terminal_exited');
    });
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, id).takeover).toMatchObject({
      terminal: 'closed',
      terminalSessionId: null,
      terminalPid: null,
    });
  });

  it('has no takeover on a workstream nobody took over', () => {
    expect(getWorkstream(store.db, running()).takeover).toBeNull();
  });

  it('lists each takeover with its own terminal', () => {
    const alpha = running('Alpha');
    const beta = running('Beta');
    store.write((ctx) => {
      enterTakeover(ctx, alpha, null, record());
      enterTakeover(ctx, beta, null, record({ providerSessionId: 'provider-2' }));
    });
    const terminal = session(alpha, 'terminal', 'provider-1');
    store.write((ctx) => {
      setSessionProcess(ctx, terminal.id, 4242, 'Wed Oct 22 12:00:00 2026');
    });
    expect(
      listWorkstreams(store.db, { states: ['TAKEN_OVER'] }).map((w) => [
        w.id,
        w.takeover?.providerSessionId,
        w.takeover?.terminal,
        w.takeover?.terminalPid,
      ]),
    ).toEqual([
      [alpha, 'provider-1', 'open', 4242],
      [beta, 'provider-2', 'closed', null],
    ]);
  });
});

describe('latestConversation', () => {
  it('is the newest started conversation and its account, or null before any started', () => {
    const id = running();
    expect(latestConversation(store.db, id)).toBeNull();
    session(id, 'managed', 'provider-1');
    session(id, 'managed', null);
    session(id, 'managed', 'provider-2');
    session(id, 'managed', null);
    expect(latestConversation(store.db, id)).toEqual({
      providerSessionId: 'provider-2',
      accountId: project.accountId,
    });
  });
});

describe('latestConversation after a move between accounts', () => {
  it("reads the account the conversation ran on, which can differ from the project's", () => {
    const id = running();
    const other = seedAccount(store, { kind: 'claude_subscription', label: 'Client' });
    session(id, 'managed', 'provider-1');
    session(id, 'terminal', 'provider-1', other.id);
    expect(listSessions(store.db, id).map((s) => [s.mode, s.accountId])).toEqual([
      ['managed', project.accountId],
      ['terminal', other.id],
    ]);
    expect(latestConversation(store.db, id)).toEqual({
      providerSessionId: 'provider-1',
      accountId: other.id,
    });
  });
});

describe('commitShasRecordedAfter', () => {
  it('lists the commits other workstreams recorded after a seq, and nothing else', () => {
    const mine = running('Alpha');
    const other = running('Beta');
    const before = latestEventSeq(store.db);
    const emit = (entityId: WorkstreamId, type: EventType, sha: string) => {
      store.write((ctx) => {
        ctx.emit({ type, entityType: 'workstream', entityId, data: { sha, paths: [] } });
      });
    };
    emit(other, EventType.CommitRecorded, 'b'.repeat(40));
    emit(mine, EventType.CommitRecorded, 'c'.repeat(40));
    emit(other, EventType.HandoffRecorded, 'd'.repeat(40));
    expect([...commitShasRecordedAfter(store.db, before, mine)]).toEqual(['b'.repeat(40)]);
    expect([...commitShasRecordedAfter(store.db, latestEventSeq(store.db), mine)]).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the repository functions**

In `packages/db/src/store/workstreams.ts`:

- import `SessionMode`, `TerminalStatus`, `type TakeoverDto` and `type TakeoverRecord` from `@10db/contracts`;
- add `readonly takeover?: TakeoverRecord | null;` to `WorkstreamPatch`;
- add `takeover: null,` to the row literal in `insertWorkstream`;
- give `toDto` a third parameter, `sessions: ReadonlyMap<SessionId, TerminalView> = currentTakeoverSessions(db, [row])`, and add `takeover: row.takeover ? takeoverDto(row.takeover, row.currentSessionId ? sessions.get(row.currentSessionId) : undefined) : null,` after `stepIndex`. `getWorkstream` and `insertWorkstream` keep calling `toDto(db, row)`; a row with no takeover runs no extra query.
- in `listWorkstreams`, keep the select, filters and order, then read the takeover sessions once for all rows: `const rows = …all(); const sessions = currentTakeoverSessions(db, rows); return rows.map((r) => toDto(db, r, sessions));`
- add:

```ts
/** What a takeover's terminal status is read from. */
type TerminalView = Pick<typeof schema.sessions.$inferSelect, 'id' | 'mode' | 'pid' | 'endedAt'>;

/** The current sessions of the taken-over rows among `rows`, in one query, by id. */
function currentTakeoverSessions(
  db: DbReader,
  rows: readonly WorkstreamRow[],
): ReadonlyMap<SessionId, TerminalView> {
  const ids = rows.flatMap((r) => (r.takeover && r.currentSessionId ? [r.currentSessionId] : []));
  if (ids.length === 0) return new Map();
  const found = db
    .select({
      id: schema.sessions.id,
      mode: schema.sessions.mode,
      pid: schema.sessions.pid,
      endedAt: schema.sessions.endedAt,
    })
    .from(schema.sessions)
    .where(inArray(schema.sessions.id, ids))
    .all();
  return new Map(found.map((s) => [s.id, s]));
}

/** The stored takeover, plus whether its terminal runs, read from the workstream's current session. */
function takeoverDto(record: TakeoverRecord, session: TerminalView | undefined): TakeoverDto {
  const live =
    session?.mode === SessionMode.Terminal && session.endedAt === null ? session : undefined;
  return {
    ...record,
    terminal: live === undefined ? TerminalStatus.Closed : TerminalStatus.Open,
    terminalSessionId: live?.id ?? null,
    terminalPid: live?.pid ?? null,
  };
}

/**
 * A terminal takes the conversation over (spec §5.4): the lease is released and the claims stay with
 * the workstream, so no agent moves into the areas the builder is editing. A rescued workstream's
 * caller has taken its claims back in the same write. The takeover service opens the terminal
 * session afterwards.
 */
export function enterTakeover(
  ctx: WriteContext,
  id: WorkstreamId,
  fence: number | null,
  record: TakeoverRecord,
): WorkstreamDto {
  transitionWorkstream(ctx, id, {
    to: WorkstreamState.TakenOver,
    fence,
    patch: { takeover: record, currentSessionId: null },
    reason: 'the builder took the session over in a terminal',
  });
  clearLease(ctx, id);
  return getWorkstream(ctx.tx, id);
}
```

In `packages/db/src/store/sessions.ts` (import `isNotNull` from `drizzle-orm` and `type AccountId` from `@10db/contracts`):

`insertSession` already takes the account a session launches on (the accounts plan's Task 9); a terminal passes its conversation's. Add:

```ts
/** Records the process a terminal session runs as, once its start time is read. */
export function setSessionProcess(
  ctx: WriteContext,
  id: SessionId,
  pid: number,
  pidStart: string,
): void {
  ctx.tx
    .update(schema.sessions)
    .set({ pid, pidStart, updatedAt: ctx.now })
    .where(eq(schema.sessions.id, id))
    .run();
}

/**
 * The provider conversation the workstream ran last and the account it ran on, or null when no
 * session has started one. The conversation's transcript lives in that account's config directory.
 */
export function latestConversation(
  db: DbReader,
  workstreamId: WorkstreamId,
): { readonly providerSessionId: string; readonly accountId: AccountId } | null {
  const row = db
    .select({
      providerSessionId: schema.sessions.providerSessionId,
      accountId: schema.sessions.accountId,
    })
    .from(schema.sessions)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(schema.sessions.workstreamId, workstreamId),
        isNotNull(schema.sessions.providerSessionId),
      ),
    )
    .orderBy(desc(schema.sessions.sequence))
    .get();
  return row?.providerSessionId
    ? { providerSessionId: row.providerSessionId, accountId: row.accountId }
    : null;
}
```

In `packages/db/src/store/events.ts` (import `and`, `eq`, `gt`, `ne` from `drizzle-orm` where missing, and `EventType`, `type WorkstreamId` from `@10db/contracts`):

```ts
/** SHAs that workstreams other than `except` recorded through the commit tool after `afterSeq`. */
export function commitShasRecordedAfter(
  db: DbReader,
  afterSeq: number,
  except: WorkstreamId,
): Set<string> {
  const rows = db
    .select({ data: schema.events.data })
    .from(schema.events)
    .where(
      and(
        gt(schema.events.seq, afterSeq),
        eq(schema.events.type, EventType.CommitRecorded),
        ne(schema.events.entityId, except),
      ),
    )
    .all();
  return new Set(
    rows.flatMap((r) => {
      const sha = r.data['sha'];
      return typeof sha === 'string' ? [sha] : [];
    }),
  );
}
```

### Task 3: Git: whether a commit is still in history, and the paths a commit changed

**Files:**

- Modify: `apps/daemon/src/git/git.ts`
- Test: `apps/daemon/src/git/git.test.ts` (a new `describe`)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: the existing `Git`, `createGit()`, `Sha` and `GitError`.
- Produces, on `Git`:
  - `isAncestor(repo: string, ancestor: string): Promise<boolean>`: true when `ancestor` is `HEAD` or in its history. It is false when a reset, rebase or amend dropped the commit, when the commit is only on another branch, and when git doesn't know it (exit 128) or it isn't a full SHA. Other failures throw `GitError`.
  - `commitFiles(repo: string, sha: string): Promise<string[]>`: the paths one commit changed against its first parent; every path for a root commit; for a merge, the paths it brought in against its first parent. It uses `git diff-tree --diff-merges=first-parent` (git 2.31 or later): `diff-tree -m --first-parent` still lists a merge's diff against every parent (checked on git 2.50.1).

```ts
describe('history checks for a takeover', () => {
  let repo: TempDir;
  beforeEach(() => {
    repo = createTempRepo();
  });
  afterEach(() => {
    repo.cleanup();
  });
  const git$ = (...args: string[]) =>
    execFileSync('git', ['-C', repo.path, ...args], { stdio: 'pipe' })
      .toString()
      .trim();
  const commitFile = (path: string): string => {
    mkdirSync(dirname(join(repo.path, path)), { recursive: true });
    writeFileSync(join(repo.path, path), `${path}\n`);
    git$('add', path);
    git$('commit', '-q', '-m', `Add ${path}`);
    return git$('rev-parse', 'HEAD');
  };

  it.each([
    { name: 'HEAD itself', setup: () => git$('rev-parse', 'HEAD'), expected: true },
    {
      name: 'an earlier commit on the branch',
      setup: () => {
        const earlier = git$('rev-parse', 'HEAD');
        commitFile('src/a.txt');
        return earlier;
      },
      expected: true,
    },
    {
      name: 'a commit a reset dropped',
      setup: () => {
        const dropped = commitFile('src/b.txt');
        git$('reset', '-q', '--hard', 'HEAD~1');
        return dropped;
      },
      expected: false,
    },
    {
      name: 'a commit only on another branch',
      setup: () => {
        git$('switch', '-q', '-c', 'side');
        const elsewhere = commitFile('src/c.txt');
        git$('switch', '-q', 'main');
        return elsewhere;
      },
      expected: false,
    },
    { name: 'a SHA the repo has never seen', setup: () => 'f'.repeat(40), expected: false },
    { name: 'something that is not a SHA', setup: () => 'HEAD~1', expected: false },
  ])('isAncestor: $name', async ({ setup, expected }) => {
    const sha = setup();
    expect(await createGit().isAncestor(repo.path, sha)).toBe(expected);
  });

  it("lists the paths one commit changed, the root commit's too", async () => {
    const git = createGit();
    expect(await git.commitFiles(repo.path, git$('rev-list', '--max-parents=0', 'HEAD'))).toEqual([
      'README.md',
    ]);
    commitFile('src/api/one.txt');
    const sha = commitFile('src/api/two words.txt');
    expect(await git.commitFiles(repo.path, sha)).toEqual(['src/api/two words.txt']);
  });

  it('lists what a merge brought in against its first parent', async () => {
    git$('switch', '-q', '-c', 'side');
    commitFile('src/ui/side.txt');
    git$('switch', '-q', 'main');
    commitFile('src/api/main.txt');
    git$('merge', '-q', '--no-ff', 'side', '-m', 'Merge side');
    expect(await createGit().commitFiles(repo.path, git$('rev-parse', 'HEAD'))).toEqual([
      'src/ui/side.txt',
    ]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `apps/daemon/src/git/git.ts`, add to the `Git` interface:

```ts
  /** True when `ancestor` is HEAD or in its history; false once a reset, rebase or amend dropped it. */
  isAncestor(repo: string, ancestor: string): Promise<boolean>;
  /** The paths one commit changed against its first parent (a merge's too); every path for a root commit. */
  commitFiles(repo: string, sha: string): Promise<string[]>;
```

Move the environment `git()` passes into a function the new method can share, `const gitEnv = (): NodeJS.ProcessEnv => ({ ...process.env, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: '0', LC_ALL: 'C' });`, and use it in `git()`. Then add to the object `createGit()` returns:

```ts
    async isAncestor(repo, ancestor) {
      const sha = Sha.safeParse(ancestor);
      if (!sha.success) return false;
      const args = ['merge-base', '--is-ancestor', sha.data, 'HEAD'];
      try {
        await run('git', ['-C', repo, ...args], { env: gitEnv() });
        return true;
      } catch (error) {
        const code =
          typeof error === 'object' && error !== null && 'code' in error ? error.code : null;
        // 1: not an ancestor. 128: git doesn't know the commit (garbage-collected, or never here).
        if (code === 1 || code === 128) return false;
        throw new GitError(args, String(error));
      }
    },
    async commitFiles(repo, sha) {
      // A merge's paths are what it brought in against its first parent; plain diff-tree lists none for it.
      const args = ['diff-tree', '--no-commit-id', '--name-only', '-r', '--root'];
      return (await git(repo, [...args, '--diff-merges=first-parent', '-z', Sha.parse(sha)]))
        .split('\0')
        .filter(Boolean);
    },
```
